- SA-129 · 2026-09-23 · WEB DRIVER MODE 1b (owner ruling: phones get the redesigned web; R1 = "view + complete"; Lane F builds after #1274 lands). SPEC, on the existing endpoints only — `POST /route-runs/:id/stops/:stopId/complete` and `/complete-with-payment` (routes.controller.ts :186, :210; both `@Roles(OPERATOR, DRIVER)`, both take the `idempotency-key` header, the second guarded by `DriverPaymentsGuard` on the body's `payment.amount`), no new API, no client-side money. PRECONDITIONS, in order: #1292 (the reconcile runs on every close), #1274 round 7 (the evidence basis), B785 (the per-visit contract + a server-projected `deliveredSoFar` per line, SA-116), B810 (`payableInvoices[].balanceDue`, SA-125). "Delivered in full" and "Refused" ship on B785; the collection button ships on B810. **(1) "Delivered in full" — the payload.** The line list is the run read the app already uses, `GET /route-runs/:id` → `stops[].orders[].lineItems` (RUN_LINE_ITEMS_SELECT: id, productId, qty, status, boxes/pieces/unitsPerBox …), plus B785's `deliveredSoFar`; the web filters `status !== CANCELLED`, and for EVERY line of EVERY order at the stop sends one entry `{ orderItemId, type: "DELIVERED", quantityDelivered: qty − deliveredSoFar }` — explicit, per visit (SA-109/109a/116); on a fresh stop that is `qty`. The web never omits an order (SA-109: zero entries = undelivered) and never sends a partial set. The call is `/complete-with-payment` with NO `payment` field when nothing is collected (the "on account" close — DriverPaymentsGuard passes a body without a payment; this is the path that reconciles on every close after #1292); the bare `/complete` is not used (SA-111 retires it). **(2) "Refused."** Per line, `{ orderItemId, type: "REFUSED", quantityDelivered: 0 }` for EVERY line of EVERY order at the stop — never a stop-level status (there is none on the wire; an order with zero entries would read as undelivered, not refused, and would not be marked DELIVERED under B785). A reason is REQUIRED: the stop-level `driverNote` must be non-empty (the DTO field exists; the web disables Refused until typed) — a refusal is the evidence the office disputes most; per-line `note` stays optional. **(3) Collection.** Only when `GET /tenants/me/addons` reports the `driver_payments` addon (the same read the app's `useDriverPayments` makes) — otherwise no amount field and no method picker; the payload is `payment: { amount, method }` with method ∈ {CASH, CHECK} for R1 (the enum values), amount = what the driver RECEIVED as typed, integer cents at most (2dp), > 0 (RF-006 refuses a zero physical payment — the web does not send a payment object for zero). The "amount due" shown beside the input is SERVER-QUOTED: Σ `balanceDue` over the stop's orders' `payableInvoices` (B810) — the same set `recordDeliveryPaymentInTx` applies the money to (SA-122) — summing server-provided numbers is arithmetic, not pricing; the web never derives a total from lines, tiers or packs. Over-payment: the server applies oldest-first across the stop's payable invoices and books the surplus as an AdvancePayment referenced `RUN:<runId>:STOP:<stopId>` — the web sends no token (the server derives it from the route params) and shows the response's `excess` ("$X saved as advance") and a confirm BEFORE sending when received > due (a display decision, not money). **(4) Idempotency.** The server keys `IdempotencyKey` rows by hash(key, `completeStop:<runId>:<stopId>` / the with-payment scope) and REPLAYS the cached response on a repeat — so the web mints ONE UUID per stop attempt when the sheet opens, sends it as `idempotency-key` on every try, disables the button while in flight, and on a network error retries with the SAME key: a replay returns the first result, a genuine second completion never happens. A `400 "Stop is already completed"` on a retry with a NEW key is the signal the first attempt landed — the web treats it as success only when its own key was reused; with a new key it refreshes the run and shows the stop's state. Every 4xx body's `message` (the SA-107b/104a stable codes where present) is shown verbatim; no offline queue — offline, the buttons are disabled with "reconnect to complete". **(5) Multi-order and re-delivery.** A multi-order stop is IN scope for the two whole-stop actions (every order full, or every order refused — B762's contract, one payload); a MIXED outcome is partial → "Finish in the app". A stop where any line has `deliveredSoFar > 0` (a re-delivery, or a reopened partial) is refused by the web for R1 and sent to the app — until B803 lands the remainder plan there, the web must not claim a remainder it cannot show. **(6) units_v1.** A unit-aware line's stored `qty` is already base pieces (`invoice-unit-line.ts` :17 / `unit-line-build.ts`), `quantityDelivered` is in that same basis, `deliveryTypeForQty(qty, qty)` = DELIVERED and the fit check bounds by `qty` — so the full-qty entry is SAFE; the web does not refuse unit lines. RED-FIRST PINS (Lane F, server-side where they bite; web where stated): (a) a two-order stop, "Delivered in full" → 2 × every line's entries, each DELIVERED at `qty − deliveredSoFar`; both orders DELIVERED; the reconcile ran once per order (SA-102); (b) "Refused" → every line REFUSED/0, every order DELIVERED with a $0 reconcile (SA-109 pin 2), `driverNote` required (a web pin: the button is disabled while empty; a server pin is not owed — the DTO allows an empty note); (c) on-account close → no `payment` object, the reconcile runs, nothing paid (the #1292 red); (d) CASH 60 on a due of 60 → applied 60, excess 0; CASH 80 on 60 → applied 60, excess 20 booked as an advance referenced RUN:<run>:STOP:<stop>, the web shows it; CASH 0 → RF-006 400, the web never sends it; (e) the addon absent → the web never renders the amount field and `payment` is never sent; present → CASH/CHECK only; (f) double-tap → one completion, the second click blocked; retry with the same key after a dropped response → the cached response, no second mutation row set (count the DeliveryMutation rows: exactly one per line); (g) a line with `deliveredSoFar > 0` → the web shows "Finish in the app" and sends nothing; (h) a unit-aware line (Case, qty 288 base) → DELIVERED 288, accepted, billed as one Case; (i) the amount due shown equals Σ `balanceDue` from the run read, and a SENT invoice from an earlier visit with 40 paid on 100 contributes 60 (SA-125's pin). Sequence: 1a (tier 2, the unit-picker lane) is independent; 1b's "Delivered in full"/"Refused" after B785, its collection after B810.

- SA-124g · 2026-09-23 · #1310 round 4 — THE ONE PRICE RULE for every replace-all caller, and the R1 scope. Opus's four new findings at 1c615285 are accepted: (1) CRITICAL — the staff controller calls the fold without the live map, `packDiffersFromLive` stays false, the STANDARD price is dropped, and `updateOrderItems` re-prices through `resolveBuyerLinePrice`, which returns the LIVE `pricePerUnit` and ignores the resolved pack — 1 box of 12 at $36 becomes 1 box of 12 at $18 after any operator auto-merge; the buyer wiring is inert because the CUSTOMER branch ignores `unitPrice`/`unitsPerBox`; (2) HIGH — a plain product with two existing lines 409s "at packs []" (the 2+ buckets branch with no pack > 1 falls to the refusal), breaking auto-merge and cart submit for common products; (3) MED — a carried price is minted as a MANUAL override; (4) LOW — a bare qty on the staff path is read as pieces. Four rounds, each adding a money path: the fold has been deciding PRICE, and price belongs in one place. **R-PRICE (one rule, every path — sweep, forceConsolidate, staff replace-all, buyer replace-all):** a line written FROM an existing line carries that line's stored `unitPrice`, `priceType`, `originalPrice` and `overrideReason` BY `sourceLineId` — never the live `pricePerUnit`, never a tier ladder at the live pack, never minted as MANUAL — with ONE exception, today's R0: when the line's resolved pack EQUALS the live pack and its `priceType` is derived (not MANUAL), the merged quantity re-derives its price as it does today. A line with NO source (a brand-new product on the order) takes the create-path price at the live pack. Mechanically: the fold carries FACTS only — `sourceLineId` (the bucket's first existing line) and the bucket's own `unitsPerBox` — and no longer decides price (`packDiffersFromLive` and its `overrideOk` clause are REMOVED from the fold); the rebuild in `updateOrderItems` is the one decider: it resolves the pack (SA-124e's order), looks the source line up BY ID, and applies R-PRICE — and the CUSTOMER branch honours a carried `sourceLineId` the same way before it falls to `resolveBuyerLinePrice` (buyer pricing applies only to a line without a source, or with a source at the live pack and a derived priceType). The sweep and forceConsolidate already obey R-PRICE through `buildMergeLineWrites` (the primary's stored price/priceType) — unchanged. **DUPLICATES:** a product whose existing buckets carry NO known pack (all ≤ 1 — a plain product, or unresolved) folds an incoming line into the FIRST bucket in display order and never refuses; the 409 exists only for two KNOWN packs > 1 that disagree with a KNOWN incoming pack. **BARE QTY on the staff path:** B673's rule — a bare qty on a boxed product is BOXES — applied through the same `normalizeBareIncomingSellingUnits` the buyer path already uses, never pieces. *_THE GATE — a *.db.spec that pins the WHOLE chain, fold → updateOrderItems → the stored row, on real Postgres, no hand-built payloads:*_ an order carrying a 12-pack line ($36, derived priceType), a 6-pack line ($18) and TWO plain-product duplicate lines; the product repacked to live 6 at pricePerUnit $18: (i) staff scan-merge of one 6-pack → the 6 line becomes 2 boxes at $18, priceType unchanged; the 12 line is recreated as 1 box / unitsPerBox 12 / $36 with its ORIGINAL priceType, never MANUAL, never $18; (ii) the buyer CUSTOMER branch submitting the same cart → byte-identical rows to (i); (iii) a plain scan → the first duplicate grows, the second is byte-identical, no 409; (iv) a bare qty 2 of the boxed product → 2 boxes at the line's pack; (v) the sweep with a 12-pack loser → merged into the 12 line at $36, pack 12; (vi) forceConsolidate the same; every assertion on the STORED columns (qty, boxes, pieces, unitsPerBox, unitPrice, priceType, overrideReason). **R1 SCOPE:** correctness over speed. Recommended: land the one-rule version above WITH the chain spec green. FALLBACK, acceptable and safer if round 4 is not green in one round: R1 = the sweep + forceConsolidate fix (done, pinned — the hourly, automatic, CRITICAL path) + the fold's refusal on a genuine known-pack disagreement + duplicates preserved; the replace-all REBUILD reverted to master byte-for-byte plus ONE fail-closed guard — a payload item whose own resolved pack (> 1) differs from the live pack (> 1) is refused with MERGE_PACK_MISMATCH instead of re-split (SA-124e's option (b), now acceptable because the owner's prod report reads 0 of 5 pending boxed lines mismatched, so the "un-mergeable after a repack" dead end is theoretical today); the correct rebuild with R-PRICE and the chain spec becomes R2. The fleet lead picks on the round-4 result; either way the chain spec is the merge gate for whichever rebuild ships.

- SA-131 · 2026-09-23 · #1321 (B756, S-scan — findOneRun's legacy unlinked-orders fallback folded a customer's DELIVERED history from ANY run into this run's stop and quoted it to the driver) @ 04c974da, two rulings for the round. Grounded: the fallback (routes.service.ts ~:1255-1288) fetches `order.findMany({ customerId in stopCustomers, status notIn [...] })` for stops with no linked orders and the result becomes `stop.orders` on the wire — which the mobile door quote sums (payment.tsx) — while the payment path itself pays only the stop's linked orders that are not CANCELLED and not DRAFT (:2583); `NON_ROUTABLE_ORDER_STATUSES` = [DRAFT, DELIVERED, CANCELLED] (routes.constants.ts :123) is what dispatch refuses to link; PARTIALLY_DELIVERED is routable. **(1) Yes — the DELIVERED exclusion applies only while the run is not terminal, and a terminal run's fallback resolves by EVIDENCE, never by a customer-wide status sweep.** Two audiences: for a PENDING/IN_PROGRESS run the fallback substitutes for the linkage dispatch would have made, so its set is exactly the routable set — `status: { notIn: NON_ROUTABLE_ORDER_STATUSES }` — nothing delivered, nothing drafted, nothing cancelled; for a COMPLETED/CANCELLED run (F11's past runs) the stop's orders are the ones that carry a `DeliveryMutation` row with `routeRunStopId = stop.id` — the same evidence rows SA-093a/113b make the delivery basis — any status; that is exact, and it is what "this run delivered" means. A seeded/legacy past run with no mutation rows shows no orders — never a by-customer DELIVERED sweep (B756's bug, which "any run's history" would reproduce on past runs); if the demo tenant's past runs must render, the demo seed writes the mutation rows (it is test data — approved tenants only), not the query. Money never reads a terminal run's fallback (no completion is possible on it), so the display split carries no exposure. **(2) In THIS PR, not a separate filing: switch the non-terminal filter to `notIn: NON_ROUTABLE_ORDER_STATUSES`.** It is the same line and the same bug class — the fallback feeding the door quote a set the payment path will not pay (DRAFT excluded at :2583 but included in the quote → an over-quote whose surplus lands as an advance); PARTIALLY_DELIVERED and OUT_FOR_DELIVERY stay in. Pins: (a) an active run's unlinked stop with a customer holding PENDING, DRAFT, DELIVERED and CANCELLED orders → the fallback returns the PENDING order only; (b) PARTIALLY_DELIVERED is returned; (c) a COMPLETED run's stop → exactly the orders with a mutation row for that stop, whatever their status, and none without; (d) a COMPLETED run's stop with no mutation rows → an empty list, never the customer's DELIVERED history; (e) the door quote on (a) equals the PENDING order's due only. The DB spec the lead's gate requires: warranted here in any case — the terminal branch is a new relation traversal (`deliveryMutation` by `routeRunStopId`), so the spec executes both branches on real Postgres. Landing: tier 1, after the Opus round.
- SA-132 · 2026-09-23 · #1322 (B825 — the Google sign-in's browser flow returns to the initiating origin for the Expo mobile-web build; B826 — the web Dockerfile bakes NEXT_PUBLIC_MOBILE_WEB_URL) @ 0325e55392fcb7fa9ae22099fd06ddc64624d12d (eight files, +505 −20; tier 1 security; Opus in parallel) — **PASS on content with ONE required change, small.** What holds, read at the sha: (1) the origin is an EXACT allow-list — `resolveReturnOrigin` returns the candidate only when it `===` `webUrl` or `mobileWebUrl`, else `webUrl`; no prefix, no substring, no regex, so the suffix-attack lookalike falls back (pinned), and a request with no candidate resolves to `webUrl` — the Next web client sends nothing, so its flow is byte-identical (pinned). (2) The resolved origin is minted INTO the OAuth state at `GET /auth/google` and the state is HMAC-signed (B349: HKDF-derived key, `timingSafeEqual`), so `profile.returnOrigin` at the callback is tamper-proof (a tamper pin covers the new field) and the callback re-validates it anyway — defence in depth, not a second trust boundary. (3) The cancelled/invalid-state paths PEEK the unverified state and pass the peeked value through the same allow-list before it is a redirect target — an evil or lookalike peeked origin bounces to `webUrl` (pinned), a legitimate mobile-web one bounces there (pinned), and `state_invalid` takes the same path (pinned). (4) The native deep-link flow (`mobile=1`) never resolves a return origin — the scheme wins (pinned). (5) The redirect target is always `<allowed origin>/auth/google/callback` with fixed error strings — no open redirect, no reflection; with Google unconfigured the endpoint 503s before any origin handling (F2's compose gate). (6) Both mobile callers (operator and buyer-standalone) pass `window.location.origin`, which the server validates — the client never chooses its own target. **REQUIRED: the allow-list must be CONFIG-ONLY.** `mobileWebUrl` falls back to a HARDCODED production origin (`https://routeflowmobile-production.up.railway.app`) when `MOBILE_WEB_URL` is unset — so every environment that never configured the variable (dev, compose, CI, a future staging) carries the production mobile origin in its allow-list, and a dev API will happily send a browser to the production callback page with a dev-minted code (the exchange fails there — no token exposure — but an allow-list entry nobody configured is not an allow-list). Rule: no default — unset means the list is `webUrl` only (the pre-B825 behaviour, fail-closed), with a boot warning like the existing `WEB_URL`-localhost one; the production API sets `MOBILE_WEB_URL` explicitly at deploy (runbook line), and the Dockerfile's `NEXT_PUBLIC_MOBILE_WEB_URL` default (a link target for the web, not an allow-list) may stay but the build arg is set explicitly per environment. Pin: `MOBILE_WEB_URL` unset + the production mobile origin as `return_origin` → `webUrl`, and the warn fires once at boot. Recommended, not required: compare origins normalised through `new URL(x).origin` on both sides (a configured trailing slash or `:443` would otherwise silently fall back to the web flow — fail-closed, but a UX failure nobody would diagnose). Landing: the head does not contain master's tip — rebase; delta at the new sha with the one change.

- SA-089a · 2026-09-23 · [AMENDS SA-089 (1)/(3); HOLD on #1324] #1324 (B660 PR B — resolving a parked regulated reversal with an operator-supplied pack) @ 4498c50e0ded5efabccdcf02bcf8aa9bdc5f65ff. What matches SA-089: the DTO (`pack` integer 1..10000, `reason` ≥ 3 chars, nothing else — pinned); the conditional one-way claim (`updateMany({ id, resolvedAt: null })`) inside ONE `tenantTransaction` with the correction, the REVERSAL and the `resolvedEntryId` stamp; `alreadyResolved` no-op; a lost claim never calls the ledger; 404 for another tenant; the CANCELLED-return refusal AFTER the claim inside the tx (the throw rolls the claim back — pinned); the semantic validation through `classifySaleAxis` with the supplied pack replacing the line's `unitsPerBox`, or standing in as the whole shape when the line is gone (pinned); `unknown` → 409 `PARKED_REVERSAL_UNRESOLVABLE`, still parked (pinned); the SHARED `buildLineRemainingBalances` + `bookReversalChunk` — no second netting (SA-E26 satisfied); never a SALE-row rewrite, never a pack written onto OrderItem/InvoiceItem; the audit action and meta as listed; the endpoint `POST /regulated/parked-reversals/:id/resolve` + the list, OPERATOR/TENANT_ADMIN, addon-gated. **Opus's HIGH stands and it amends my SA-089 (1):** "legacy → ONE correction row of `+qty×(pack−1)` on the SALE, then book" is wrong whenever part of the line was already reversed on the BOX axis (a LINE_UNIT return booking `unitBasis = rowRemainingUnitBasis`, a credit note at ratio 1, a void's aggregate): the sale's basis becomes `qty×pack` while those reversals still carry `−r` units, so the line's remaining reads `(qty×pack) − Σr` instead of `(qty − Σr)×pack` — `Σr×(pack−1)` phantom units; on a FULLY voided 5-box line resolved at 12, the correction adds +55, the remaining reads 55 units at $0, the booker writes −5 at $0, and the filing ends +50 units where it was 0 — and the spec at :2850 pins that wrong figure with an impossible prior. RULINGS: (a) YES — "closed" is decided from the remaining qty AND net BEFORE any correction row: `remQty ≤ ε` and `remNet ≤ ε` on the box axis → resolve and close, `resolvedEntryId` null, NO correction row, NO reversal row, audit `bookedPieces 0` "line already fully reversed" (SA-089 (3) as written; the order of operations was mine to state and I did not). (b) RESTATE BOTH, so the whole line moves to the piece axis and SA-070 hazard 2 stays closed: the SALE's correction is the full `+qty×(pack−1)` (so `classifySaleAxis` reads the sale as piece-axis forever after), AND one correction per earlier BOX-axis reversal row `r` (a REVERSAL-typed correction, `correctsEntryId` = that reversal, `originalSoldAt` = its `soldAt`, `soldAt` now, qty 0, `unitBasisQty = −|r|×(pack−1)`, net 0, tax 0) — a reversal is box-axis when `|unitBasisQty| == |qty|` against a legacy sale (a fractional credit-note ratio is a fractional r; the formula holds). After both, remaining units = `(qty − Σr)×pack`, remaining net unchanged, and every row of the line agrees on one axis; then book `min(returnedPieces, remaining)` through the shared booker as now. The alternative — correcting only the un-reversed boxes (`qtyRem×(pack−1)`) — leaves the SALE's own basis at neither `qty` nor `qty×pack`, which the classifier would read as unknown and PARK again on the next return (hazard 2 reopened); rejected. (c) CONCURRENCY: the resolution reads the sale, its corrections and the line's remaining, then writes, with no lock on the line — a concurrent live return, credit note or void on the same order reads the same remaining and both book. Take the SAME lock the live reversal path (`reverseReturnEntries`) takes around its read-then-book; if the live path takes none, wrap the resolution's `tenantTransaction` in the order-keyed advisory lock (`withAdvisoryLock`, family `order-merge`, key `orderId`, mode wait — SA-093(B)'s shape) and FILE the live path's missing lock as its own item; never a new lock family, never a second lock inside an existing one. A `SELECT … FOR UPDATE` on the sale row alone is not enough — the remaining is a sum over the line's rows, not one row. ALSO REQUIRED: the *.db.spec over the new writes (the lead's gate) — real Postgres for (i) legacy + fully voided → closed, zero rows written, filing unchanged; (ii) legacy 5 boxes, 2 already returned as LINE_UNIT (−2 units), resolved at 12 with 24 returned pieces → the SALE correction +55, ONE reversal correction −22, remaining 36 units, booked −24, the line ends 12 units; (iii) legacy, one credit note at ratio 0.5 → the fractional correction; (iv) the classifier re-reads the sale as piece-axis after (ii) and a later return does NOT park (hazard 2); (v) two concurrent resolutions/returns on the line serialise on the lock (the SA-093(B) pin shape). And the two SA-089 red-first pins still missing from the branch: cancel after resolve → the REVERSAL gone, the parked row still resolved, the correction rows present; `prepareFiling` passes after resolution. Landing check: cite the `tobacco_dealer` entry in `addon-gate-registry.ts` (my grep found none — the registry spec would be red if it is truly absent, so name the line). Delta at the new sha.

- SA-138 · 2026-09-24 · [SPEC, per-tenant POLICY layer phases 1–2] Full text: `.claude/sa/POLICY-LAYER-SPEC.md`. ONE RULE pinned: policy = the tenant about itself (TENANT_ADMIN writes; platform only under impersonation, audited), never read by a guard, no key overlaps FEATURE_REGISTRY — `disabled` is a behaviour the tenant can flip back, not a kill switch. B18's DRAFT→Issue flow verified DEAD (CreditNoteStatus ISSUED|APPLIED|VOID, no issue route) — the credit pilot stands alone: band limit < exposure ≤ limit+T → accepted + pending stamps, not routable (409 CREDIT_APPROVAL_PENDING), approve/reject endpoints, delivery action never widened; no new status enum value. driverAtDoorEdit: amend = today's delta merge (:5318-5346), replace = absolute requestedQty (mutation = requested − held), disabled = 409 for DRIVER at create; short-pick is evidence, out of scope. priceDisclosure: set ⊆ {originalPrice, discount, msrp}, enforced at the SOURCE (portal payloads, invoice-pdf-item.ts, email.service.ts null the hidden fields), office PDF unaffected (pinned). - SA-089b · [AMENDED by SA-171: Return FOR UPDATE first, then the SOURCE Order, then the CANCELLED check, then the claim — cancel locks the CARRYING order (inline-returns :610), which can differ from the parked row's source order, so the Return row is the lock both paths share; earlier SA-167 amendment superseded] 2026-09-24 · [AMENDS SA-089a(c), #1324] The live reversal paths lock the ORDER ROW (inline-returns.service.ts create :191 `SELECT … "Order" … FOR UPDATE` → reverseLedgerForReturn :313; cancel :593 Return → :610 Order → unreverseReturnEntries :650). An advisory lock (order-merge) does not exclude a row lock. RULING: the parked-reversal resolution takes the Order row FOR UPDATE as the first statement after its conditional claim, inside its tenantTransaction, before reading sale/corrections/remaining — not withAdvisoryLock. Lock order: resolution = Order only; cancel = Return → Order; no cycle. Pin (v): live create + resolution on one line started together serialise on the Order row. - SA-139 · 2026-09-24 · [RULING, B841 HIGH] inline-return `approve()` (:487-545) locks only the Return row (:516) and reverses at :545 with no Order lock → two approvals on one order, or approve vs create/cancel/resolution, both book against the same remaining. RULE: approve takes the carrying Order FOR UPDATE after its Return lock, before reverseLedgerForReturn (Return → Order, the order cancel already uses at :593/:610 — no deadlock), and re-reads the return's items after the lock. Real-Postgres pins: two approvals on two returns against one line with remaining for one → the second books min(remaining, requested) / refuses via the shared booker, never both in full; approve vs create serialised.
